pytorch/pytorch ↗ 1 release · 1 stable
v2.15.0PyTorch 2.15.0 Release Stable2026-10-28T17:09:49Z

PyTorch 2.15.0 Release Notes

  • Highlights
  • Backwards Incompatible Changes
  • Deprecations
  • New Features
  • Improvements
  • Bug fixes
  • Performance
  • Documentation
  • Security
  • Developers

Highlights

<table> <tr><td><strong>Rewritten NCCL backend becomes the default</strong>, with a flight recorder that dumps on hangs and <code>TORCH_DIST_USE_NCCL2=0</code> or <code>nccl-legacy</code> as rollback paths</td></tr> <tr><td><strong>One-sided transports for <code>torch.distributed</code></strong>, with a NIXL backend and CUDA-graph-capturable transfers</td></tr> <tr><td><strong>FSDP2 and pipelining gain finer memory and communication control</strong>, with fp32 for precision-sensitive parameters, CUDA-graph-captured gradient accumulation, and gradient reduction overlapped with compute</td></tr> <tr><td><strong>Faster 4-bit (NVFP4) LLM decoding on NVIDIA Blackwell GPUs</strong> through fewer, better-overlapped kernel launches in NVGEMM</td></tr> <tr><td><strong>Apple Silicon gains FP8 and faster decode</strong>, with <code>float8_e4m3fn</code> and <code>_scaled_mm</code>, a new <code>grouped_mm</code> with a decode fast path (1.32–1.34x tokens/s on MoE decode), and public <code>torch.mps.Stream</code> APIs</td></tr> <tr><td><strong>Broader platform support</strong> with FlyDSL kernels on AMD gfx950, Tensor Data Mover support for gfx1250 (MI450), ROCm 10.0 wheels, simpler SYCL kernel authoring and multi-GPU profiling on Intel GPUs, CUDA 13.2 as the default wheel with CUDA 13.4 alongside it, and <code>torch.compile</code> on Python 3.15</td></tr> </table>

For more details about these highlighted features, you can look at the release blogpost. Below are the full release notes for this release.

Backwards Incompatible Changes

Python Frontend

import torch can no longer be re-executed in the same process, including via importlib.reload(torch) (#194654)

importlib.reload(torch) and del sys.modules["torch"] + re-import now raise, as does retrying import torch after it failed. Re-running the module body was already unsafe in exactly the same way as the retry, so the new check does not distinguish the two. Normal use is unaffected: a successful import leaves torch in sys.modules so the body never re-runs, and re-entrant imports from a backend extension during autoload still short-circuit on sys.modules without reaching the check.

Version 2.14:

import importlib
import torch

importlib.reload(torch)
# RuntimeError: Only a single TORCH_LIBRARY can be used to register the namespace triton; ...
# (re-importing after a failed `import torch` failed with similarly misleading errors or could segfault)

Version 2.15:

import importlib
import torch

importlib.reload(torch)
# ImportError: `torch` can only be initialized once per process, so this module cannot
# be imported again: neither via `importlib.reload(torch)`, nor by re-importing after
# a failed `import torch`.

Nested tensors are no longer allowlisted for torch.load(..., weights_only=True) (#194380)

Nested jagged and strided nested tensors are no longer on the default weights_only allowlist. Loading a checkpoint that contains one with weights_only=True (the default) now fails with Unsupported global: GLOBAL torch.nested._internal.nested_tensor._rebuild_njt was not an allowed global by default (or GLOBAL torch._utils._rebuild_nested_tensor for strided nested tensors). To load checkpoints containing nested tensors, allow them explicitly with torch.serialization.safe_globals (see below), or use weights_only=False for trusted files.

Version 2.14:

loaded = torch.load("njt.pt", weights_only=True)  # works

Version 2.15:

from torch.nested._internal.nested_tensor import _rebuild_njt, NestedTensor
with torch.serialization.safe_globals([_rebuild_njt, NestedTensor]):
    loaded = torch.load("njt.pt", weights_only=True)

Operators called through torch.ops.* and torch._C._dispatch_call_boxed now use PyTorch's own warning and exception translation instead of pybind11's (#193451)

We make torch.ops.* calls go through the same exception and warning translation as the rest of the library. This means that some exception translation that used to be handled by pybind is now handled natively. User-defined pybind exception translation is thus lost. Restoring it may be done in a future release as part of a broader exception handler improvement.

In addition, TORCH_WARN warnings from these operators now reach Python's warnings module, so they raise when warnings are turned into errors (-W error). std::out_of_range still becomes IndexError (#195713).

Version 2.14:

# C++ op: throw std::invalid_argument("bad size");
try:
    torch.ops.my_lib.my_op(x)
except ValueError:  # pybind11 translation
    ...

Version 2.15:

# C++ op: TORCH_CHECK_VALUE(false, "bad size");
try:
    torch.ops.my_lib.my_op(x)
except ValueError:
    ...

# TORCH_WARN from the op is now a Python warning
with warnings.catch_warnings():
    warnings.simplefilter("ignore")
    torch.ops.my_lib.my_op(x)

Autograd

Selective activation checkpointing now saves, rather than recomputes, the outputs of operators with an ordered effect unless the policy returns CheckpointPolicy.MUST_RECOMPUTE (#196956)

In eager mode, if a create_selective_checkpoint_contexts policy returns CheckpointPolicy.PREFER_RECOMPUTE for a non-aliasing operator with an ordered effect (e.g. via register_effect(torch.library.EffectType.ORDERED)), that choice is now overridden and its outputs are saved, so the side effect is no longer replayed during backward. This can increase activation memory. To keep the 2.14 behavior, return CheckpointPolicy.MUST_RECOMPUTE for that operator. Operators in the c10d namespace are excluded. Under torch.compile, CheckpointPolicy.MUST_RECOMPUTE on an effectful operator now raises at compile time: RuntimeError: AOTAutograd does not support MUST_RECOMPUTE for effectful operations because forward effects cannot join the backward effect-token chain. Use a save policy instead.

policy = lambda ctx, op, *args, **kwargs: CheckpointPolicy.PREFER_RECOMPUTE
checkpoint(fn, x, use_reentrant=False, context_fn=lambda: create_selective_checkpoint_contexts(policy))
# 2.14: an effectful op in fn runs twice; 2.15: once (use CheckpointPolicy.MUST_RECOMPUTE to replay)

Distributed (c10d)

The nccl backend now uses a rewritten, cleaned-up NCCL implementation by default (#192281, #192895, #193237, #193241)

backend="nccl" (also the default CUDA backend of init_process_group()) now creates the new implementation. The legacy implementation is available as backend="nccl-legacy" for this release only: nccl-legacy and TORCH_DIST_USE_NCCL2=0 will be removed in the next release, so migrate to nccl or nccl-lazy.

Use nccl (eager initialization) by default. Use nccl-lazy when you need independent point-to-point communicators: collectives run on one eagerly created communicator, and each send/recv peer pair gets its own lazily created communicator and stream, so concurrent P2P to different peers can overlap (as in the legacy backend).

Differences from the legacy backend:

  • Communicators are created eagerly in init_process_group()/new_group(), allocating GPU memory even for unused groups.
  • Each group is bound to one CUDA device: device_id, else LOCAL_RANK % torch.cuda.device_count() (or the global rank modulo the device count); torch.cuda.set_device() is not used. Tensors on another GPU fail with Expected tensor on cuda:X but found tensor on cuda:Y or ProcessGroupNCCL is bound to device cuda:X but an operation was issued on cuda:Y. Pass device_id if needed; several GPUs per process requires nccl-legacy.
  • dist.get_backend_impl(...) returns the new backend class, so isinstance(..., ProcessGroupNCCL) is False and legacy-only methods such as comm_split_count() are missing.
  • An async all_gather's work.get_future() value is the list of per-rank outputs, not a one-element list holding a stacked tensor; read the output list passed to all_gather instead (works on both backends).

To temporarily restore the legacy behavior in 2.15, set TORCH_DIST_USE_NCCL2=0 on every rank before creating the process group, or use nccl-legacy:

Version 2.14:

dist.init_process_group("nccl")  # ProcessGroupNCCL

Version 2.15:

dist.init_process_group("nccl", device_id=torch.device("cuda", local_rank))  # new implementation
dist.init_process_group("nccl-lazy", device_id=torch.device("cuda", local_rank))  # independent P2P communicators
dist.init_process_group("nccl-legacy")  # legacy implementation, removed in the next release

Distributed Checkpointing

Distributed checkpoint metadata now records tensor strides, so checkpoints saved with PyTorch 2.15 cannot be loaded by PyTorch 2.14 or earlier (#194251)

TensorProperties gains an optional strides field in the pickled .metadata file. In older versions dcp.load raises a CheckpointException reporting too many values to unpack (expected 5). There is no flag to write the old format.

Version 2.14 (loading a 2.15 checkpoint):

dcp.load(state_dict, checkpoint_id="/ckpt/step_100")
# CheckpointException: ... too many values to unpack (expected 5)

Version 2.15:

dcp.load(state_dict, checkpoint_id="/ckpt/step_100")  # loads 2.14 and 2.15 checkpoints

Distributed FSDP2

With MixedPrecisionPolicy(reduce_dtype=None), fully_shard now accumulates and reduces gradients in the parameter's grad_dtype (its original dtype by default) instead of in param_dtype (#194434)

This is BC-breaking: mp_policy.reduce_dtype=None no longer defaults to mp_policy.param_dtype.

  • [popular case] fp32 params, MixedPrecisionPolicy(param_dtype=bf16)
    • before: grad accumulation and reduce happen in bf16
    • after: grad accumulation and reduce happen in fp32 (2x reduce-scatter bytes)
  • [rare case] bf16 params, MixedPrecisionPolicy(param_dtype=fp32)
    • before: grad accumulation and reduce happen in fp32
    • after: grad accumulation and reduce happen in bf16

How to deal with BC: explicitly set mp_policy.reduce_dtype.

In grad accumulation (no reduce-scatter), the unsharded param's .grad always holds the accumulated value, visible via model.parameters() while unsharded. Before this change, .grad was None when reduce_dtype was set and differed from param_dtype (FSDP kept the sum in a hidden reduce_dtype buffer).

Version 2.14:

# fp32 parameters
mp_policy = MixedPrecisionPolicy(param_dtype=torch.bfloat16)
fully_shard(model, mp_policy=mp_policy)
# gradients accumulate and reduce-scatter in bf16

Version 2.15:

# fp32 parameters
mp_policy = MixedPrecisionPolicy(param_dtype=torch.bfloat16)
fully_shard(model, mp_policy=mp_policy)
# gradients accumulate and reduce-scatter in fp32

# to keep the 2.14 behavior, set reduce_dtype explicitly
mp_policy = MixedPrecisionPolicy(param_dtype=torch.bfloat16, reduce_dtype=torch.bfloat16)
fully_shard(model, mp_policy=mp_policy)

Distributed Pipelining

torch.distributed.config.pipeline_per_direction_p2p is renamed to pipeline_per_edge_p2p and now uses one communicator per directed rank-to-rank edge (#196666)

The old names were removed with no alias: setting the attribute raises AttributeError: torch.distributed.config.pipeline_per_direction_p2p does not exist, and TORCH_DISTRIBUTED_PIPELINE_PER_DIRECTION_P2P is silently ignored.

Version 2.14:

dist_config.pipeline_per_direction_p2p = True

Version 2.15:

dist_config.pipeline_per_edge_p2p = True  # or TORCH_DISTRIBUTED_PIPELINE_PER_EDGE_P2P=1

Profiler

C++ stack traces are now symbolized with dladdr instead of addr2line by default (#195462)

TORCH_DISABLE_ADDR2LINE now defaults to on, since addr2line is slow and can hang. C++ frames in TORCH_SHOW_CPP_STACKTRACES=1 errors and CUDA memory snapshots now show the library path and offset, plus the symbol name only if exported (otherwise ??). To restore addr2line, set TORCH_DISABLE_ADDR2LINE=0 or TORCH_SYMBOLIZE_MODE=addr2line.

TORCH_SHOW_CPP_STACKTRACES=1 TORCH_DISABLE_ADDR2LINE=0 python train.py

CUDA memory snapshots record the allocator's mallocWithAddress tag in a new internal_metadata field instead of appending it to user_metadata (#193432)

user_metadata in torch.cuda.memory._snapshot() trace entries is now exactly what was set with torch.cuda.memory._set_memory_metadata (which now also accepts a dict). Tools that searched user_metadata for mallocWithAddress must read internal_metadata instead.

# 2.14
tagged = [e for e in trace if "mallocWithAddress" in e["user_metadata"]]
# 2.15
tagged = [e for e in trace if e.get("internal_metadata") == "mallocWithAddress"]

Remove the deprecated FunctionEvent.metadata_json property (#198265)

Accessing it now raises AttributeError: 'FunctionEvent' object has no attribute 'metadata_json', and the metadata_json constructor argument is removed. Use FunctionEvent.metadata, a dict populated when _ExperimentalConfig(expose_kineto_event_metadata=True) is set (not the deprecated event_metadata).

metadata = event.metadata_json  # 2.14: JSON string
metadata = event.metadata  # 2.15: dict[str, Any] or None

Rename the experimental CUPTI monitor profiler backend to Cuspy, without aliases for the old names (#195881)

In _ExperimentalConfig(custom_profiler_config=...), "cupti_monitor" is now "cuspy" and "cupti_monitor_async_export" is now "cuspy_async_export"; old names silently fall back to the default Kineto backend. prof.take_pending_cupti_export() is now take_pending_cuspy_export() (the old name raises AttributeError). Prefer the new torch.profiler.CuspyConfig.

# 2.14
cfg = _ExperimentalConfig(custom_profiler_config='{"backend":"cupti_monitor"}')
# 2.15
cfg = _ExperimentalConfig(custom_profiler_config='{"backend":"cuspy"}')

Dynamo

Higher-order ops now reject input mutation and aliasing in their bodies unless the op opts in (#185738)

Before, Dynamo let any higher-order op body mutate or alias its inputs by default. Ops that do not implement those semantics could silently produce invalid graphs. Mutation and aliasing are now off by default. The built-in wrappers that support them (wrap, checkpoint, autograd.Function, autocast/set-grad wrappers) opt in. A flex_attention mask_mod may read captured views but must not mutate inputs or return aliases. Under fullgraph=True, unsupported mutation or aliasing raises Encountered input mutation during higher order op tracing or Encountered aliasing during higher order op tracing; otherwise, Dynamo graph breaks and falls back to eager execution. Rewrite the higher-order op body to avoid mutation or aliasing.

For example, rewrite an in-place flex_attention score modifier as an out-of-place operation:

Version 2.14:

def score_mod(score, b, h, q, k):
    score.add_(1)
    return score.clone()

Version 2.15:

def score_mod(score, b, h, q, k):
    return score + 1

Inductor

torch.compiler.nested_compile_region(options=...) now accepts only an allowlist of Inductor config patches (#195864, #195865)

Patches passed through get_invoke_subgraph_compile_options(fw_inductor_config_patches=..., bw_inductor_config_patches=...) used to accept any torch._inductor.config key, though many were silently ignored. Only fallback_by_default and max_autotune are now accepted; other keys raise ValueError: Inductor config key '<key>' is not supported in forward nested compile-region options (or backward). Apply other options to the whole compiled function. Patch dictionaries are now snapshotted when the options are created, so later mutation has no effect, and the default no longer implicitly sets triton.autotune_at_compile_time=True.

Version 2.14:

opts = get_invoke_subgraph_compile_options(fw_inductor_config_patches={"triton.multi_kernel": 1})

Version 2.15:

opts = get_invoke_subgraph_compile_options(fw_inductor_config_patches={"max_autotune": True})
compiled = torch.compile(fn, options={"triton.multi_kernel": 1})

torch.compiler.precompile is now a module instead of a callable; capture with torch.compiler.precompile.capture(...) and reload with torch.compiler.precompile.load(...) (#197993, #198135, #198213)

Calling torch.compiler.precompile(fn, *example_inputs) now raises TypeError: 'module' object is not callable. capture(fn, *, artifact_path, cache_path) returns a context manager that you call once inside the with block; it writes both files when the block exits cleanly (or on cap.save()), and load now takes those paths. decompositions= moves to tracer=MakeFxTracer(decompositions=...).

Version 2.14:

python_code, cache = torch.compiler.precompile(lambda m, x: m(x), model, x)
f = torch.compiler.precompile.load(python_code, cache)

Version 2.15:

with torch.compiler.precompile.capture(lambda m, x: m(x), artifact_path="m.py", cache_path="m.cache") as cap:
    cap(model, x)
f = torch.compiler.precompile.load("m.py", "m.cache")

Composability

Under functionalization, a regenerated output of split, split_with_sizes or unbind is now rebuilt as a single slice or select of its base, so its grad_fn is SliceBackward0/SelectBackward0 instead of SplitBackward0/SplitWithSizesBackward0/UnbindBackward0 (#194037)

This greatly reduces compile time for code that mutates many split() views (for example 32s to 15s with 128 views). Gradients are unchanged, but the in-place error now names the new op with output index 0: RuntimeError: Output 0 of Select is a view and is being modified inplace. Code that inspects grad_fn names, matches the old error text, or pattern-matches split/unbind nodes in functionalized graphs needs updating. There is no option to restore the previous behavior.

Version 2.14:

>>> out = torch.func.functionalize(f)(x)  # f returns base.unbind(0)[1] after base.add_(1)
>>> out.grad_fn
<UnbindBackward0 object at 0x...>

Version 2.15:

>>> out = torch.func.functionalize(f)(x)
>>> out.grad_fn
<SelectBackward0 object at 0x...>

Release Engineering

PyPI wheels now ship with CUDA 13.2, and CUDA 12.6 and CUDA 13.0 wheels are no longer published (#196249, #198913)

The default PyPI wheel moves from CUDA 13.0 to CUDA 13.2. The 2.15 wheels are cu132 and cu134; cu126 and cu130 are no longer built. CUDA 13 wheels need NVIDIA driver 580 or newer and a GPU of Turing (sm_75) or newer, so Maxwell, Pascal and Volta GPUs and hosts with a CUDA 12.x driver should stay on 2.14.

Version 2.15:

# PyPI wheel now uses CUDA 13.2
pip install torch

# CUDA 13.4 wheels
pip install torch --index-url https://download.pytorch.org/whl/cu134

# To stay on CUDA 12.6 or CUDA 13.0, pin PyTorch 2.14:
pip install torch==2.14.0 --index-url https://download.pytorch.org/whl/cu126  # or cu130

Python 3.10 wheels are no longer published (#198233, #199398)

PyTorch 2.15 raises the minimum supported Python version for binaries to 3.11: no cp310 wheels are built for torch or the Triton wheels, and CI no longer tests on Python 3.10. On Python 3.10, pip cannot find a 2.15 wheel. Upgrade to Python 3.11 or newer, or stay on PyTorch 2.14.

Version 2.14:

python3.10 -m pip install torch  # cp310 wheel available

Version 2.15:

python3.11 -m pip install torch==2.15.0  # Python 3.11 through 3.15 (including 3.14t and 3.15t)

ROCm wheels move to ROCm 7.14 and 10.0; ROCm 7.2 wheels and gfx900/gfx906 kernels are dropped (#194919)

gfx900 (MI25) and gfx906 (MI50/MI60, Radeon VII) users can stay on 2.14 (rocm7.2) or build from source with those targets in PYTORCH_ROCM_ARCH.

pip install torch --index-url https://download.pytorch.org/whl/rocm7.14  # or rocm10.0

Linux CUDA wheels no longer depend on the cuRAND library (#194850)

PyTorch only uses the cuRAND device API, so torch no longer links libcurand, no longer preloads it on import torch, and the wheel's CUDA requirements no longer include cuRAND. Extensions or libraries that call the cuRAND host API (for example curandCreateGenerator) and relied on pip install torch bringing in the cuRAND library can fail to load libcurand.so and must now install it themselves.

Version 2.15:

pip install torch
pip install "cuda-toolkit[curand]==13.2.2"  # match the toolkit version of your wheel

CUDA

torch.histc(..., out=...) on CUDA now raises an error when out does not have the same dtype as the input (#196100)

CUDA used to silently cast into any out dtype. It now matches CPU and MPS and rejects mismatches, even widening ones. Compute without out and cast instead.

Version 2.14:

>>> torch.histc(x, bins=4, min=-2.0, max=2.0, out=int64_out)  # silently truncated

Version 2.15:

RuntimeError: torch.histogram: input tensor and hist tensor should have the same dtype, but got input float and hist long int
>>> int64_out.copy_(torch.histc(x, bins=4, min=-2.0, max=2.0))  # workaround

On CUDA, cuBLAS and cuBLASLt workspaces are now allocated for each operation instead of being cached per handle and stream (#194311)

Graphs captured separately on one stream could share the cached workspace and fail on replay with CUDA error: an illegal memory access was encountered. Workspaces are now freed after each op, so torch.cuda.memory_allocated() excludes them and the handle from torch.cuda.current_blas_handle() has no ATen workspace attached. Set TORCH_CUBLAS_WORKSPACE_CACHE=1 to restore caching (unsafe with multiple graphs on one stream). TORCH_CUBLASLT_UNIFIED_WORKSPACE now only takes effect with caching enabled. ROCm has a matching change (#196920).

Version 2.14:

>>> torch.mm(a, a, out=out)
>>> torch.cuda.memory_allocated() - before > 0  # workspace stays allocated
True

Version 2.15:

>>> torch.cuda.memory_allocated() - before  # freed after the op
0

CPU (AArch64)

Float32 torch.sin and torch.cos on Arm CPUs (NEON and SVE) now use SLEEF's 3.5-ULP kernels instead of the 1.0-ULP kernels, matching the accuracy of the x86 AVX2 implementation and running 1.7x-2.4x faster on Neoverse V1/V2 (#196315)

Float32 sin and cos results on Arm can now differ from earlier releases in the last bits. Bit-for-bit comparisons against stored golden outputs, torch.equal, or torch.testing.assert_close with rtol=0, atol=0 may fail; default assert_close tolerances are unaffected. There is no switch to restore the previous kernels; if you need tighter accuracy, compute in float64 and cast back.

Version 2.14:

>>> x.cos()  # float32 on Arm: within 1 ULP

Version 2.15:

>>> x.cos()  # float32 on Arm: within 2 ULP
>>> x.double().cos().float()  # workaround: correctly rounded

MPS

The MPS backend now requires macOS 15 (Sequoia) or newer (#199261, #200290)

macOS 14 has reached end of life, so its MPS workarounds were removed. On macOS 14, torch.backends.mps.is_available() returns False and allocating on mps raises the error below. To keep using MPS, stay on PyTorch 2.14 or upgrade macOS; CPU builds still run on older macOS. C++ extensions: at::mps::MacOSVersion::MACOS_14_4 was removed.

>>> torch.ones(2, device="mps")  # 2.15 on macOS 14
RuntimeError: The MPS backend is supported on macOS 15.0+. Current OS version can be queried using `sw_vers`

XPU

Remove the allocator property from torch.xpu.MemPool, aligning with other accelerators (#192041)

torch.xpu.MemPool no longer exposes the allocator it was constructed with, so pool.allocator now raises AttributeError. Keep your own reference to the allocator instead; passing one to the MemPool constructor is unchanged.

Before:

pool = torch.xpu.MemPool(allocator.allocator())
my_allocator = pool.allocator

After:

my_allocator = allocator.allocator()
pool = torch.xpu.MemPool(my_allocator)

Complex Frontend

Ops that are undefined for complex inputs now raise TypeError instead of RuntimeError (#192350)

This covers order-dependent ops (sort, argsort, topk, argmax/argmin, max/min with dim, maximum/minimum, fmax/fmin, aminmax), isposinf/isneginf, view_as_real/imag on real tensors, and view_as_complex on complex tensors, matching Python (1j < 2j is a TypeError). except RuntimeError: no longer catches these; catch (RuntimeError, TypeError). Ops not yet implemented for a dtype (e.g. rad2deg, nansum, heaviside on complex, topk/argmax on bool) now raise NotImplementedError, which except RuntimeError: still catches. Full max()/min()/amax/amin are unchanged.

torch.sort(torch.randn(3, dtype=torch.cfloat))
# 2.14: RuntimeError:  Sort does not support complex dtypes on CPU
# 2.15: TypeError:  Sort does not support complex dtypes on CPU

Deprecations

Optimizer

Passing a bare set of parameters to an optimizer is deprecated and now emits a FutureWarning; it will become an error in a future release (#198710)

Set iteration order can change between runs, so optimizer state could silently mismatch parameters on reload. The warning reads: optimizer parameters need to be organized in ordered collections, but the ordering of tensors in sets will change between runs. Please use a list instead.

opt = torch.optim.Adam(set(model.parameters()))  # 2.15: FutureWarning
opt = torch.optim.Adam(list(model.parameters()))  # recommended

Linear Algebra Frontend

MAGMA is deprecated for LU factorization with pivoting on CUDA: torch.linalg.lu_factor, torch.linalg.lu, and the ops that use it internally (torch.linalg.solve, torch.linalg.inv, torch.linalg.det, torch.linalg.slogdet) now always use cuSOLVER (#190661)

This only affects code that sets torch.backends.cuda.preferred_linalg_library("magma"): pivoted LU now uses cuSOLVER anyway; a new built-in batched LU kernel replaces MAGMA's for large batches. When MAGMA is preferred, the first call warns: linalg.lu_factor: MAGMA, as a linear algebra backend, is deprecated and will be removed in future releases. pivot=False still uses MAGMA when it is preferred. To avoid the warning, use the default or cuSOLVER backend.

torch.backends.cuda.preferred_linalg_library("magma")
LU, pivots = torch.linalg.lu_factor(A)  # 2.14: MAGMA; 2.15: cuSOLVER + UserWarning
torch.backends.cuda.preferred_linalg_library("default")  # recommended

Profiler

torch.autograd.profiler.load_nvprof, parse_nvprof_trace and EnforceUnique are deprecated and will be removed in PyTorch 2.17 (#198396)

nvprof does not support recent GPUs. They now emit a FutureWarning such as `torch.autograd.profiler.load_nvprof` is deprecated and will be removed in PyTorch 2.17. Use torch.profiler.profile or Nsight Systems with torch.autograd.profiler.emit_nvtx().

events = torch.autograd.profiler.load_nvprof("trace.prof")  # 2.14
with torch.profiler.profile() as prof:  # 2.15
    run_workload()

FunctionEvent.event_metadata is deprecated in favor of FunctionEvent.metadata (#198405)

metadata is a typed dict[str, Any] with everything event_metadata had. Accessing event_metadata emits FutureWarning: event_metadatais deprecated. Usemetadata instead.

grid = event.event_metadata.grid  # 2.14
grid = event.metadata["grid"]  # 2.15

Inductor

Deprecate torch._inductor.config.triton.use_block_ptr because Triton removed its block-pointer API (#191007)

With a Triton that no longer provides block pointers, use_block_ptr=True is now a no-op that falls back to the default indexing path with a one-time FutureWarning: config.triton.use_block_ptr=True but the installed Triton no longer provides the block-pointer frontend API, instead of failing with CompilationError: ... Block pointers have been removed in favor of the tensor descriptor API. Use tensor descriptors instead.

Version 2.14:

torch._inductor.config.triton.use_block_ptr = True

Version 2.15:

torch._inductor.config.triton.use_tensor_descriptor = True

Deprecate the no-op torch._inductor.config.use_mixed_mm and torch._inductor.config.mixed_mm_choice settings (#193721)

These settings have been ignored since the tuned mixed_mm path was removed. Reading or setting either now emits a one-time FutureWarning such as torch._inductor.config.use_mixed_mm is deprecated and does not do anything. autoheuristic_collect.mixed_mm and autoheuristic_use.mixed_mm were removed, and "mixed_mm" is no longer the TORCHINDUCTOR_AUTOHEURISTIC_USE default. Delete these settings from your code.

Version 2.14:

torch._inductor.config.use_mixed_mm = True  # silently ignored

Version 2.15:

# FutureWarning; delete the line

Rename torch._inductor.config.benchmark_epilogue_fusion to benchmark_template_fusion and max_epilogue_benchmarked_choices to max_template_fusion_benchmarked_choices (#198430)

The settings also cover prologue fusion, so the old names were misleading. The old names remain as deprecated aliases that emit a one-time FutureWarning. The environment variable is now TORCHINDUCTOR_BENCHMARK_TEMPLATE_FUSION; TORCHINDUCTOR_BENCHMARK_EPILOGUE_FUSION is still read as a fallback.

Version 2.14:

torch._inductor.config.benchmark_epilogue_fusion = False

Version 2.15:

torch._inductor.config.benchmark_template_fusion = False

CUDA

torch.cuda.graph_annotations.clear_kernel_annotations() is deprecated and now emits a FutureWarning (#195398)

The CUDA graph kernel annotation registry now cleans up after itself: annotations are rekeyed to the executable graph when it is instantiated and dropped when that graph is destroyed. A global clear also discards annotations for graphs that are still live. Remove the call, and reset or delete a graph to drop its annotations.

Version 2.14:

annotations = get_kernel_annotations()
clear_kernel_annotations()

Version 2.15:

annotations = get_kernel_annotations()
# No clear needed; g's annotations are dropped when g is reset or destroyed.

New Features

torch.nn

  • Add torch.nn.functional.scaled_addmm and in-place scaled_addmm_, which compute beta * input + alpha * scaled_mm(mat1, mat2) in a single cuBLASLt GEMM on CUDA with the same scaling recipes as scaled_mm; also supported under torch.compile (#195409, #195410)

Optimizer

  • Add torch.optim.functional, a public module of functional optimizers (adam, adamw, sgd, muon and others) that run the same implementations as the optimizer classes while the caller manages parameters, gradients and state (#194986)

Autograd

  • Add ctx.input_grad_buffers to custom torch.autograd.Function backward, exposing the engine's gradient accumulation buffers (or None when unavailable) so a fused kernel can accumulate an input's gradient in place and return None for it. Availability depends on backward order, so keep an out-of-place fallback; buffers must be updated synchronously within the current backward call, and the property is unavailable with create_graph=True, anomaly detection, or post-hooks on the producing node (#196491)
  • Add torch.autograd.graph.queue_callback(callback), a public API to run a callback when the current backward pass finishes (#193958)

Distributed (c10d)

  • Add an experimental one-sided transport API, torch.distributed._transport, with a NIXL backend and CUDA-graph-capturable stream-ordered transfers (#195643, #197032, #198889, #198918)
  • Add an experimental pure-Python watchdog, torch.distributed._watchdog, that detects hung CPU and GPU operations in Python distributed backends and dumps thread stacks on timeout (#192075)
  • Add get_peer_cft_handle(peer) and get_multimem_cft_handle() to NCCL symmetric memory for NCCL Compute Fabric Transport (#191685)
  • Add ProcessGroupNCCL.Options.lazy_init so nccl and nccl-lazy groups create their communicator on first use instead of allocating ~200 MiB in new_group (#200020)

Distributed Checkpointing

  • FsspecReader and FsspecWriter are now public in torch.distributed.checkpoint, and FsspecWriter supports dcp.async_save through a new cache_staged_state_dict argument placed before overwrite (pass overwrite and later arguments by keyword); importing the old private _fsspec_filesystem module emits a DeprecationWarning (#188698)

Distributed FSDP2

  • Add FSDPModule.set_manual_backward_finalization(True) and FSDPModule.finalize_backward() to run gradient reduction and resharding at a chosen point instead of in the autograd final callback (#196640)
  • Add finalize_backward(async_op=True), which returns a GradientReductionHandle so gradient reduction can overlap other compute (#196724)
  • Add FSDPModule.set_keep_unsharded_storage() to keep unsharded parameter storage allocated across reshard() (#198417)
  • Let tensor-subclass parameters free raw all-gather buffers right after fsdp_post_all_gather by implementing fsdp_should_release_all_gather_outputs_after_post_all_gather() (#194114)

Distributed Pipelining

  • Add PipelineStage.register_forward_context(factory) to wrap each stage forward in a context manager that receives the stage and microbatch indices (#196462)
  • Add torch.distributed.pipelining.analyze_pipeline_activation_liveness, which computes activation lifetimes and reusable storage slots from a schedule (#196464)

DeviceMesh

  • Add the experimental DeviceMesh.abort() to abort all process groups of a root mesh, avoiding hangs when a peer exits early (#189655)

Profiler

  • Add torch.profiler.ProfilerActivityConfig and torch.profiler.PerformanceMetricsConfig to configure collection per activity group, such as CUDA hardware performance counters (#196065)
  • Add torch.profiler.CuspyConfig to enable the experimental Cuspy CUDA collection backend without _ExperimentalConfig (#196066)

Dynamo

  • Add torch.compiler.config.static_sources (TORCH_COMPILE_STATIC_SOURCES), the inverse of dynamic_sources, listing sources that stay static even under automatic dynamic shapes, PGO, or dynamic=True, but not explicit mark_dynamic (#193626)
  • Let custom backends define a _dynamo_backend_init callable that runs when torch.compile() resolves the backend, so out-of-tree backends can load native libraries or set up devices and fail fast (#192345)

Inductor

  • Add thread-based Inductor compile workers for free-threaded Python, selected with TORCH_COMPILE_WORKER_MODE=thread or auto to avoid process-spawn and pickling overhead (#179547, #179548)
  • Add an experimental FlyDSL GEMM autotuning backend for ROCm gfx950, covering FP16/BF16 mm, grouped_mm and MXFP4/MXFP8 scaled_mm, enabled by adding FLYDSL to torch._inductor.config.max_autotune_gemm_backends (#190903, #192877, #194032, #194303, #194981, #196719)

Ahead-Of-Time Inductor (AOTI)

  • Add num_runners and use_stream_affinity arguments to torch._inductor.aoti_load_package, which keep each non-null CUDA or XPU stream on the same model instance across calls (#194783)
  • Add an optional lifecycle observer to the C++ AOTIModelContainerRunner: attach a torch::inductor::AOTIModelContainerObserver with set_observer() to get begin/end callbacks around inference, constant swaps and constant folding (#193999)

Foreach

  • Add the public torch.foreach module (beta), which exposes the multi-tensor torch._foreach_* operations under stable, documented names (e.g. torch.foreach.mul_(params, 3)) with parameter names that follow the ordinary ops (#193607)

C++ Frontend

  • Added torch::stable::sort to the stable ABI (#198038)
  • Made more c10/util helpers available header-only under torch/headeronly/util (#194673, #194781)
  • Add stable ABI conversions for torch.dtype/torch.device Python objects and a torch::stable::is_tensor_pyobject check (#192774)

Release Engineering

  • Enable the FlashAttention backend of scaled_dot_product_attention in Windows CUDA builds and wheels for Ampere and newer GPUs. It was previously disabled on Windows, so SDPA fell back to the math backend; in a GQA benchmark (bf16, sequence length 2048) on an RTX 5090 the forward pass drops from 11.5 ms to 0.49 ms (#186343)
  • torch.compile now works on Python 3.15 (previously it raised RuntimeError: torch.compile is not supported on Python 3.15+; the check now applies to Python 3.16 and newer), and the Python 3.15 and 3.15t wheels are built against CPython 3.15.0rc2, after the ABI freeze, instead of beta releases (#178393, #199356)

CUDA

  • Add GreenContext.split and torch.cuda.green_contexts.SMPartition for creating green contexts with disjoint SM allocations, plus a GreenContext.sm_count property reporting the SMs actually allocated; requires a CUDA driver and cuda.bindings 13.1 or newer (#198278)
  • Add locality_domain_ids to GreenContext.split and SMPartition.split, together with get_num_locality_domains() and is_localization_supported(), to restrict SM partitions to hardware locality domains; requires a CUDA driver and cuda.bindings 13.4 or newer (#198279)
  • Add a "bfx9" mode for torch.backends.cuda.matmul.fp32_precision that keeps FP32 inputs and outputs but lets cuBLAS use its BF16x9 algorithm on compute capability 10.0 and 10.3 GPUs; requires a CUDA 12.9 or newer build (#195301)
  • Record the Python launch stack of each CUDA graph node during capture with annotation_config={"record_py_stacks": True}, read via torch.cuda.graph_annotations.get_kernel_py_stacks() (requires CUPTI >= 13.3) (#198419)

cuDNN

  • Support causal grouped-query attention with a paged KV cache in the cuDNN backend of torch.nn.attention.varlen (varlen_attn and varlen_attn_out), enabling head dimension 256 with a paged KV cache on Blackwell GPUs; requires cuDNN 9.24 or newer (#195650)

MPS

  • Add a public MPS stream API (torch.mps.Stream, current_stream(), default_stream(), set_stream(), the torch.mps.stream() context manager) and Tensor.record_stream on MPS (#191415, #193464)
  • Add torch.float8_e4m3fn support on MPS (#194074, #196915, #196918, #196945, #196955, #197383, #197801)
  • Support float8_e4m3fn inputs in torch.nn.functional.scaled_mm on MPS (#196916)

XPU

  • SYCL Kernel Programming Simplified: Add SYCL free-function kernel support to the XPU backend and migrate most existing SYCL kernels to it, so device code can be written as explicit free functions in a more intuitive style instead of verbose functors (#196387, #196932, #198200, #198720, #200289)
  • Enhanced XPU Runtime Capabilities: Support sharing torch.xpu.Event across processes, through torch.multiprocessing or ipc_handle() / from_ipc_handle() on an event created with interprocess=True (#191182, #192495, #193399, #192514)
  • Improved XPU Profiling Experience: Add kernel-level visibility into XPU Graph replay, cross-rank correlation for XCCL collectives, and per-kernel hardware counter collection across all Intel GPUs in multi-GPU workloads to the XPU profiler (#195128, #198200)

Improvements

Python Frontend

  • Saturate out-of-range integer scalar bounds in clamp and hardtanh instead of wrapping or raising (e.g. torch.clamp(uint8_tensor, min=-255) is a no-op) (#193169)
  • Support empty batch dimensions in FFT operations on CPU, CUDA and MPS (#190483)
  • Add skip_unsupported to FlopCounterMode to warn instead of failing on higher-order operators without a FLOP formula, and count flex_attention FLOPs (#167224)
  • Support Optional[list[Tensor]] annotations in torch.library.infer_schema and custom_op (#187012)
  • Make torch.iinfo and torch.finfo hashable (#193290)
  • Name the context manager in tracebacks through torch.no_grad-style decorators (#186502)
  • Improve type stubs for Tensor @ scalar and torch.utils re-exports (#195220, #195289)
  • Add uint16, uint32 and uint64 support to torch.clamp, torch.maximum and torch.minimum on CPU and CUDA (#189953)

torch.nn

  • Support soft-label targets with reduction="none" on the chunked linear_cross_entropy path (#188296)
  • Allow FlashAttention-4 in scaled_dot_product_attention with different query/key and value head dimensions (#193039)
  • Support deterministic bicubic F.interpolate backward (#193707)
  • Support bicubic mode for 5-D input in F.grid_sample (#194787)
  • Allow varlen_attn to dispatch causal attention to cuDNN (#193630)
  • Add weight to the F.mse_loss type stub (#196126)

Optimizer

  • Validate lambd and alpha in torch.optim.ASGD (#193303)
  • Run the optimizer graph-capture health check on all accelerators (#194362)

Autograd

  • Add backward and forward-mode AD for the torch.special Bessel functions (#189872)
  • Add forward-mode AD for F.grid_sample (#195280)
  • Add torch._functorch.config.activation_memory_budget_require_full_coverage to let region_activation_memory_budget apply to a whole compiled graph (#195932)
  • Allow reading Tensor.grad_dtype on non-leaf tensors (#190852)

Distributed

  • Make Dynamo guards rank-portable under compile_on_one_rank (#196150, #196938, #196939, #196940)

Distributed (c10d)

  • Extend the new NCCL backend: collective runtime estimation, the NCCL allocator for backend tensors, an opt-in shrink/grow reconfigure() path, legacy-matching errors, and windows and reconfiguration on ROCm (#192854, #192855, #199240, #194953, #193282, #194774)
  • Add a comm_ptr property returning the ncclComm_t on all NCCL backends (#197558, #198865)
  • Support ReduceOp.PREMUL_SUM in functional all_reduce and under LocalTensorMode, and backward for MIN/MAX (#190942, #193868, #191947)
  • Stop emitting a FutureWarning from all_gather_into_tensor and reduce_scatter_tensor (#193874)
  • Allow a CPU device_id in init_process_group (#198340)
  • Add TORCH_NCCL_TEARDOWN_ON_TIMEOUT to terminate on a legacy NCCL timeout instead of hanging (#196187)
  • Record the flight recorder for backends without built-in support (the new NCCL backend, MPI, UCC) and dump it on new NCCL backend health-check failures (#192233, #199092)
  • Add an expert-major receive layout and zero-copy dispatch/combine to the experimental token-switch MoE dispatch API (#187784, #187785)
  • Pass timeout and enable_reconfigure to dynamically created TorchComms communicators (#199099)
  • is_symm_mem_enabled_for_group() no longer requires the deprecated enable_symm_mem_for_group() call (#193115)

DTensor

  • Support torch.compile for DTensor random ops on XPU (#187969)

DistributedDataParallel (DDP)

  • Add manual backward finalization to DistributedDataParallel: set ddp.require_manual_backward_finalization = True and call ddp.finalize_backward(), so other work can overlap the in-flight gradient all-reduce (#196651)
  • Add a lazy_bucket_allocation option to DistributedDataParallel that allocates gradient buckets during backward and frees them afterward, lowering peak memory (#199110)

Distributed FSDP2

  • Add param_dtype_override_fn to MixedPrecisionPolicy for per-parameter mixed precision in fully_shard, keeping one all-gather per group (#196626)

Distributed Pipelining

  • Add WAIT_SEND_F/WAIT_SEND_B schedule actions and an optional max_outstanding_sends limit on pending sends (#196726, #196727)
  • Add a max_active_stages argument to multi-stage schedules to control how many FSDP stages stay unsharded at once (#193972)
  • Label pipeline schedule actions in CUDA graph traces captured with enable_annotations=True (#195072)

  • Support more Python in compiled regions: bytearray, types.SimpleNamespace, bytes(), calling classmethod/staticmethod, list subclasses overriding __new__, del of captured variables, and more of math, cmath, operator and heapq (#192205, #199252, #193719, #196103, #197499, #197781, #191512, #189216, #196867, #197498, #197222, #198685, #195999, #192942, #192834, #195765)

  • With allow_rnn disabled, graph break at the nn.RNN/nn.GRU/nn.LSTM call so the rest of the frame compiles (#186614)

  • Forward torch.compile(..., name=...) to custom backends as kwargs["name"] (#196229)
  • Improve diagnostics: less warning spam on backend fallbacks, carets in graph-break stacks, and clearer recompile reasons and error hints (#185155, #186249, #185466, #185419, #197464, #197450, #195691)
  • aot_compile serializes more functions (functools.wraps helpers, types.MethodType, closure cells) and prunes unpicklable values no guard reads (#196472, #196473, #196474, #196476, #196478, #196479, #196481, #196684, #196685, #196686, #196687, #196691, #196692, #197750, #197752, #197754, #197755, #197833, #197762)
  • load_compiled_function(..., f_globals=...) and loaded aot_compile modules resolve guarded globals against the live scope and re-read them on each call; a guarded global missing from f_globals fails with KeyError on G['NAME'] (#196815, #196817, #196818, #196908, #196897, #197218, #196904)
  • Make aot_compile module dispatch more robust and improve its error messages for guard mismatches, missing globals, unpicklable values and incompatible artifacts (#197055, #196826, #197047, #196823, #197149, #197048, #197220, #197050, #197257, #197319, #196816, #196693, #196819, #197391, #198061, #196470, #197756)
  • maybe_mark_dynamic accepts min/max for an initial value range, and passing only one of them to mark_dynamic no longer crashes (#194625)
  • Keep the device index of torch.cuda.synchronize() unresolved under the experimental torch.distributed.config.compile_on_one_rank mode, so the artifact is not rank-specific (#196937)

  • Add gfx1250 (MI450) to the ROCm wheel architectures and build the ROCm CI against ROCm 10.0 (#196610, #194794)

CUDA

  • Support scalar scatter for float8 dtypes on CUDA (#197199)
  • Extend CUDA graph kernel annotations: an annotation_config argument to torch.cuda.graph with a CUPTI backend (#191999), key_by="source" and "auto" modes that can annotate kernels inside nested torch.cond / torch.while_loop bodies (#196528, #196530, #197577), and a cuda_graph_annotations argument to export_chrome_trace that writes them into the trace (#194854)
  • CUDAGraph.get_graph_data() now reports kernel grid and block dimensions, dynamic shared memory size, and programmatic dependency edges (#196018, #196527)
  • Add PYTORCH_CUDA_ALLOC_CONF options expandable_segments_reserve and expandable_segments_reserve_by_class to shrink the virtual-address reservation of expandable segments (#192269), and explain these options when that reservation fails (#193211)
  • CUDA out-of-memory errors now say when a custom MemPool allocator returned a null pointer (#199363)
  • Add opt-in TORCH_CUDA_CHECK_STRAY_CONTEXT=warn|error, which makes torch.cuda.set_device warn or raise when another device already holds a CUDA primary context (#191036)

  • Make float32 sum and norm on NEON use the same accumulation order as AVX2 and SVE256, so results are bitwise identical across these CPU types; NEON results can differ from earlier releases in the last bits (#197037, #197252)

  • Fix wrong gradients in cuDNN/MIOpen RNN backward with non-contiguous saved outputs, a repeated dropout mask in multi-layer MIOpen RNNs, and segfaults in RNN cells and the packed RNN fallback (#198773, #189285, #191232, #197560)

Release Engineering

  • Fix C++ compilation failures of argmax and argmin under torch.compile on s390x by moving the ZVECTOR Vectorized implementation to 16-byte vectors; s390x CPU builds now target z15 (#173072)
  • Fix the PEP 517 source distribution being left out of the GitHub release because it exceeded the 2 GiB asset limit (the plain source archive leaked into it), and check release asset sizes on every tag (#195783)
  • Fix CUDA builds from the source distribution or release tarball failing with "No download info given for 'nccl_external'": the NCCL pin files are now shipped, and a missing NCCL now fails with a message that names the fixes (USE_SYSTEM_NCCL=1 or USE_NCCL=0) (#197544)
  • Fix ROCm 7.14 wheels printing rocSHMEM Could not open libnuma on import torch (#195672)
  • Fix Windows wheels failing to import with [WinError 1114] when an older msvcp140.dll (before 14.40) is already loaded (#196987)
  • Fix wheel license metadata listing license files for code that is not shipped, and Windows MAX_PATH failures while packaging them (#185813)
  • Fix standalone cmake --install scattering Python package files across the install prefix and writing into the source checkout (#191494)
  • Make Python 3.15 Linux wheels depend on triton, so torch.compile works on GPU after pip install torch (#200294)
  • Ship top_level.txt in wheels again, fixing slow importlib.metadata.packages_distributions() calls that delayed importing libraries like transformers (#201347)

CUDA

  • Fix silently wrong results or illegal memory accesses from 32-bit index overflow on CUDA in arange, range, linspace and logspace (#197713), cdist backward (#198452), bincount (#189685), and bmm (#196431)
  • Fix CUDA batch norm under AMP overflowing to inf (#196125)
  • Fix a deadlock when a lazy-init callback such as torch.cuda.manual_seed re-enters CUDA lazy initialization (#182948)
  • Fix device-side assertions on CUDA 13 builds failing with CUDA error: invalid argument instead of reporting the assertion (#195899)
  • Fix CUDA graph annotation bugs: mark_stream mutating the caller's dict (#194851), leaked annotations (#198418), missing backward annotations with the CUPTI backend (#194853), and get_graph_data() failing on batch memory-operation nodes (#193091)
  • Fix torch.cuda.make_graphed_callables graphs being garbage-collected during an unrelated capture (#195397)
  • Fix torch.cuda.empty_cache() unmapping expandable segments on another device without synchronizing it (#197159), and expandable segments imported over IPC each reserving about 9/8 of device memory (#198129)
  • Recover from a corrupted or truncated JITerator kernel cache entry by recompiling the kernel (#186346)
  • Fix an illegal memory access in CUDA embedding_bag backward with per_sample_weights by adding the index bounds check the forward kernels already have (#193408)
  • Fall back to the ATen implementation of outer-product bmm when the Triton driver cannot be set up, instead of failing (#197695)

cuDNN

  • Fix scaled_dot_product_attention with the cuDNN backend returning NaN query gradients on Hopper (H100/H200) for non-causal attention when the key/value sequence length is 64 modulo 128, and silently corrupting generation on B200 for models with fewer than 8 key/value heads; the wheels now ship cuDNN 9.26, and with older cuDNN versions the affected Hopper shapes fall back to another backend (#196861, #195492)

CPU (x86)

  • Fix the vectorized double to int64 conversion on AVX2 CPUs, which was only exact within +/-2^51 and rounded to the nearest integer instead of truncating toward zero; it now truncates toward zero across the full int64 range like other CPU instruction sets. Also fix the vectorized int64 to double conversion returning -0.0 for 0 when the rounding mode is round-toward-negative (#196614, #196607)

CPU (AArch64)

  • Fix contiguous float32, float16 and bfloat16 to uint8 conversions of out-of-range values (for example Tensor.byte() on -1) on Arm CPUs saturating to 0 instead of wrapping around to 255 as on x86 and for strided inputs (#196646, #198744)
  • Fix torch.compile of 4-bit dynamically quantized matmul on Arm CPUs without the dot-product extension (#197518)

Performance

Python Frontend

  • Reduce TensorIterator overhead and dtype refcount contention under free-threading (#194422, #194371)

torch.nn

  • Speed up nn.Module.__call__ by about 15% (#196236)
  • Add a channels-last CUDA GroupNorm kernel (#191945)
  • Speed up RMSNorm backward up to 2x on sm12x and chunked linear_cross_entropy by 6-10% (#192400, #196134)

Distributed (c10d)

  • Speed up all_gather_object (#196185)
  • Share the parent Store connection across split_group child groups (#196665)
  • Use the NVLS multicast (multimem) barrier for CUDA symmetric memory when a multicast mapping is available (#192861)

DistributedDataParallel (DDP)

  • Batch DDP's copy into .grad with batched_grad_copy=True (#190524)

Distributed FSDP2

  • Reduce reduce-scatter gradient memory for size-1 shard groups and low-precision gradients (#196348, #198668)

Distributed Pipelining

  • Lower peak memory and improve overlap via lazy receive buffers and new defer_reduce_grad_wait/unshard_lookahead options (#196463, #196963, #196725, #196706)

Linear Algebra Frontend

  • Add a built-in CUDA batched LU kernel that matches MAGMA, and improve LU backend selection without pivoting (#190329, #193004)

FX

  • Reduce compile time in FX from_node provenance tracking (#193051)
  • Speed up the FX accelerator splitter on large graphs (#198519, #198654)

Dynamo

  • Reduce per-call overhead of compiled code and helpers like assert_size_stride (#190623, #195951, #198019, #196771, #197451, #197733)
  • Reduce compile time: defer gen-2 GC while compiling (4.2s on one model; see gc_gen2_threshold_during_compile), skip empty graphs (custom backends are no longer called for such graphs), and speed up large functions (#193289, #193157, #195622)

Inductor

  • Reduce compile time by caching in fusion search and codegen, hashing sources in the background, and compiling combo-kernel benchmarks in parallel (#186319, #192817, #192820, #194010, #194011, #198747, #198881, #198320)
  • Allow mix-order reduction fusion for more shapes, speeding up RMSNorm backward (#186370, #188660)
  • Reduce cpp_wrapper kernel call overhead by 40-50% (#192287)
  • Improve CPU FlexAttention BF16 performance by 6-29% (#191780)
  • Use larger reduction tiles on GB300, roughly doubling HBM bandwidth use (#193774)
  • Add gfx950 FlexAttention backward configs (1.8-2.3x faster) (#194151)
  • Improve NVGEMM NVFP4 decode performance on Blackwell and widen its autotuning candidates (#194651, #194652, #197485, #195539, #198293)
  • Compute only one triangle for large BF16 X @ X.T on SM100 (#194177, #194178)
  • Speed up collective bucketing, including up to 9x faster uneven reduce-scatter padding (#196647, #196621, #195616)
  • Simplify slice_scatter chains to reduce recomputation and peak memory (#192136, #195608, #195631)
  • Fix CPU OpenMP parallelization moving onto inner reduction loops under freezing, and recover an XPU inner-reduction regression (#190928, #190986)
  • Fuse torch.cat/torch.stack copies into foreach kernels for dynamic shapes (#198294)
  • Enable nested reduction fusion by default (TORCHINDUCTOR_NESTED_REDUCTION=0 disables it), fusing patterns such as RMSNorm followed by MXFP8/NVFP4 quantization into one kernel (#190594, #190595, #191974, #193598, #196332, #196333, #196334)

Ahead-Of-Time Inductor (AOTI)

  • Parallelize pinned host staging copies when loading CUDA constants (#195257)

AOTDispatcher

  • Speed up the min-cut partitioner, reducing cold compile time by about 5% (#197724)

Composability

  • Speed up FakeTensor view cache hits (#192821)
  • Reduce compile time in ShapeEnv (#193219, #193161, #195623)

Quantization

  • Speed up AArch64 small-batch QNNPACK linear 2-4x (#193595)

Foreach

  • Reduce _foreach_norm/max CUDA scratch memory (#190549)

CUDA

  • Avoid CPU-GPU synchronization when assigning scalars through indexing on CUDA, e.g. x[mask] = 1.0 (#189303, #188330, #198811)
  • Speed up CUDA upsample_linear1d by 5-25x for small outputs (#180885), pooling backward (#191086, #195119, #196156), dtype-conversion copies (#195542, #198723), 2D transpose copies (#194310), and zero_ (#195303, #198565)
  • torch.topk on CUDA 13 Linux wheels uses ahead-of-time compiled radix-select kernels on Hopper and Blackwell (median 2.16x on H100) (#190899)
  • Fix a 10-30x slowdown of gather, index_select, x[idx] and scatter_add on rows of 16 or 32 bytes (#200995)

cuDNN

  • Prefer the cuDNN backend for SDPA on sm86/sm89 for higher performance (#192799)

CPU (x86)

  • Fix the CPU kernel dispatch cache never being populated, which made every dispatch to a CPU kernel repeat the CPU capability selection (#195328)

CPU (AArch64)

  • Optimize libtorch code layout with LLVM BOLT, using profiles shipped in the repository. The new USE_LLVM_BOLT build option is on by default for Linux aarch64 CUDA builds when llvm-bolt 21 or newer is found and USE_PRIORITIZED_TEXT_FOR_LD is enabled, giving up to 30% faster workloads in some cases and up to 3% slower in others; set USE_LLVM_BOLT=0 to opt out in source builds (#186245)
  • Use the native GEMV path for single-column (n == 1) BF16 GEMMs on Arm Neoverse CPUs built with oneDNN ACL, for about 5x faster single-thread GPT-2 BF16 decode on Graviton 4 and 5 (1.4x-2.7x with 8-64 threads on Graviton 5) (#198411)
  • Dispatch non-contiguous FP16 matmuls to oneDNN ACL GEMM kernels instead of the reference implementation on AArch64 (#198498)

MPS

  • Move more MPS ops (softmax, nll_loss, padding, where, ...) to native Metal kernels, cutting launch overhead and driver memory growth with variable shapes (#196071, #195441, #195153, #195368, #193826, #198719, #198688, #198497, #196924, #189548, #199687, #198878)
  • Speed up MPS reductions, linear algebra, GEMV, convolutions, histc, masked fill, dropout and sort (#198494, #198611, #198635, #197397, #198646, #199278, #198721, #197862, #196241, #196900, #195944, #198732, #195950, #193623, #193167, #192164, #196016, #195020, #195011, #198960, #196922)
  • Speed up softmax over a non-innermost dim and relu, clamp_min, leaky_relu and other scalar-argument unary ops on row-strided views (#199758, #199933)

XPU

  • Speed up SDPA training on XPU by aligning the oneDNN graph SDPA pattern with oneDNN v3.13 and integrating its GQA and dropout support (#195419)
  • Fuse the GroupNorm forward into a single kernel and speed up contiguous cat with vectorized stores on XPU (#194842)
  • Avoid register spilling in mixed fp32/bf16/fp16 elementwise kernels on XPU by adding vectorized type-casting loads (#194185)
  • Speed up col2im on XPU by replacing its hard-coded work-group size (#198720)
  • Reduce oneMKL LAPACK overhead on XPU by trimming scratchpad initialization, host synchronizations and re-allocation (#193110)

JIT

  • Speed up torch.jit.load and graph pre-optimization (#194035, #193107, #193250, #194009)

Documentation

Python Frontend

  • Document undefined behavior of bool tensors holding bytes other than 0/1, and clarify Tensor.index_add_'s index argument (#199012, #193403)

torch.nn

  • Fix the linear_cross_entropy docstring snippet and nn.RNN example, and document bias with probability targets (#198941, #188433, #173888)

Autograd

  • Document that custom Function/op backward gradients may have arbitrary strides and that unpack_hook order is unspecified (#195210, #198016)
  • Point torch.utils.checkpoint docs to torch_remat and drop the stale use_reentrant "version 2.9" error notice (#193998, #193720)
  • Fix math rendering on the autograd mechanics page (#192562)

Distributed

  • Document the remaining NVSHMEM bindings (#193449)

Distributed (c10d)

  • Add a tutorial for the distributed debug server (#178800)
  • Document Backend.Options and fix irecv and NCCL env var docs (#196123, #189623, #194701)

Distributed FSDP2

  • Document parameter-mutation limits of fully_shard (#198826)

TorchElastic

  • Fix the EtcdServer example and Worker docstring (#198238, #199255)

Profiler

  • Fix the toggle_collection_dynamic example (#190369)

Dynamo

  • Remove references to the obsolete dynamic_shapes config (#198707)
  • Fix the PGO config example in the dynamic shapes docs (#191027)
  • Document more torch._logging.set_logs options (#188138)

Inductor

  • Document the FlexAttention ROWS_GUARANTEED_SAFE requirement (#194154)

Foreach

  • Fix in-place foreach type stubs to return the input list (#193466)

Release Engineering

  • Fix docs search ranking module stub pages above the API symbols they document and listing duplicate results (#195042)
  • Link the build environment variable reference from README.md and CONTRIBUTING.md, and fix outdated build instructions there (#193617)
  • Document the issue and pull request workflow in CONTRIBUTING.md and add a maintainer guide to the community docs (#198527)

MPS

  • Note MPS lacks float64 support (#193545)

ROCm

  • Document gfx1151 SDPA layouts, hipFile GPUDirect Storage, and MI300 matmul precision issues (#190771, #195478, #190736)

Security

Python Frontend

  • Fix torch.load(weights_only=True) exposing uninitialized pybind11-backed objects (such as DTensor placements) when a checkpoint uses the NEWOBJ opcode without a matching BUILD; such checkpoints are now rejected as corrupt or malicious (#194647)
  • Fix a stack buffer overflow in torch_shm_manager (libshm) when the Unix socket path is too long (e.g. a long TMPDIR); it now raises an error (#195183)
  • After import torch.distributed, torch.load(weights_only=True) and get_unsafe_globals_in_checkpoint no longer treat builtin exceptions as safe globals (a process-wide allowlist added in 2.14), and loading no longer calls overridden append/extend on allowlisted list subclasses. Checkpoints with pickled exceptions now need torch.serialization.add_safe_globals; object collectives are unchanged (#200956)

Developers

Distributed (c10d)

  • Make FakeProcessGroup support all_to_all_single with any sizes and add a simulate_uniform_ranks option (#193985, #194545)
  • Fix a clang build error and add amd_smi as a ROCm 7.14+ build dependency (#194104, #192520)
  • Add the private torch.distributed._functional_collectives.wait_tensors, which waits on all outputs of a multi-output functional collective at once; torch.compile uses it for coalesced collectives and batch_isend_irecv (#193892)

Profiler

  • Kineto source now lives in third_party/kineto instead of a git submodule (#198648)
  • Remove the ignored profiler_metrics and profiler_measure_per_kernel _ExperimentalConfig arguments (now a TypeError), and deprecate the no-op adjust_profiler_step option (#198262, #193042)

FX

  • Fix type annotations on Tracer(autowrap_modules=...) and CodeGen.process_inputs (#195467, #188287)

Inductor

  • Add DeviceInterface and DeviceOpOverrides extension points for out-of-tree accelerator backends (#190326, #190614, #192896, #193908, #190615)
  • Add declarative implies support to Inductor/Dynamo Config (#196663)

Ahead-Of-Time Inductor (AOTI)

  • Add DeviceOpOverrides.aten_device_type() so backends provide their ATen device type to AOTInductor C++ codegen (#193904)

C++ Frontend

  • c10::List iterators satisfy std::random_access_iterator, and List<T>::emplace now constructs a T (#196002)

Developer Experience

  • Add spin test to run pytest or test/run_test.py like CI (#198649)

Build Frontend

  • Add an opt-in USE_CMAKE_INSTRUMENTATION=1 build option to collect build metrics (CMake >= 4.3) (#189455)
  • Turn off USE_FLASH_ATTENTION automatically when no target CUDA architecture is sm80 or newer (#194502)
  • Fix the misspelled CUDA_LIMIT_GPU_ARCHITECTURE in CUDA architecture autodetection (#195695)
  • Honor USE_MIMALLOC=OFF on Windows and AArch64 (#199064)
  • Fix the FlashAttention CUDA build on Windows with MSVC in C++20 mode (#197382)

Release Engineering

  • Move the CD wheel build scripts under .ci/wheel/ (.ci/manywheel/ to .ci/wheel/linux/, .ci/macwheel/ to .ci/wheel/mac/, and the Windows pipeline to .ci/wheel/windows/) and share one numpy build pin (#189275)
  • Add a native-AOT stage to CUDA builds that exports CuTeDSL and Triton kernels ahead of time and embeds them into libtorch_cuda. The stage skips itself when it does not apply (for example CUDA older than 13 or no supported GPU architecture), but once it decides to export, a missing nvidia-cutlass-dsl (or Triton, installable with scripts/install_triton_wheel.sh) is a hard error; set TORCH_NATIVE_AOT=0 to skip the stage (#194243, #196747, #196507)
  • Fix the CMake C and C++ compiler flag checks sharing one cache variable, so a result for one language could be reused for the other; the generated HAS_<FLAG> variables are now prefixed HAS_C and HAS_CXX (#197614)
  • Add a persistent [no-ci] PR title prefix that skips build, test and lint jobs, and make @pytorchbot merge -i ignore current failures across every PR in a ghstack stack (#196534, #197143)

CUDA

  • Update the bundled CUTLASS to v4.8.0 and CUTLASS-Operators to v0.3.0 (#198179)

CPU (AArch64)

  • Add the AT_VEC_CUSTOM_MATH compile definition, which hides the built-in vectorized math functions on AVX2, AVX512, NEON and SVE256 builds so that a vectorized math library other than SLEEF can be plugged in (#197419)

MPS

  • Fix building with clang 16 and Apple clang 15 (#199115)
  • Fix building with a Metal 4 toolchain against older macOS SDKs, and deprecation warnings with the macOS 27 SDK (#196104, #197200)
  • at::mps::MPSProfiler begin*/end* methods take the MPSStream*; stream-less begin* overloads are deprecated (#192525, #193611)
  • Fix macOS builds failing on SDKs that lack the Metal 4 cooperative-tensor APIs (#195302)
  • Fix stale embedded Metal shaders in incremental macOS builds (#196202)
  • Fix MPS shader includes in editable installs (#195295)

ROCm

XPU

  • Build XPU with pure SYCL compilation and support Clang as the host compiler; expose USE_SYCLTLA and enable oneMKL sparse in the build system (#194185, #198720, #196387, #198200)
  • Upgrade oneDNN to v3.13 (#196712)

v1.27.0-pre Prerelease2026-10-28T15:18:12Z

This week's release includes syntax highlighting for Go 1.27 generic methods, an option to open README files by default, and AI commit messages that follow your repository's recent commit style.

Features

AI

  • Improved Agent Panel memory usage by loading tool-call diffs and parsing thread message Markdown only when first viewed, instead of when loading a thread. (#65444, #65470; thanks Vanuan)
  • Improved AI commit message generation by using the three most recent repository commit messages as style examples. (#61528; thanks matheuslrd)
  • Improved CPU usage and disk reads while the agent was working, especially with a long thread history. (#66015)

Git

  • Added missing tooltips to the close, commit message, and "View on" buttons in the Git Graph's commit details panel. (#66016; thanks skmanoj322)
  • Improved Git Graph rendering performance. (#66180; thanks RemcoSmitsDev)
  • Improved the Git Panel's error message when a push was rejected because the local branch was behind the remote, explaining that remote changes must be fetched and integrated before pushing again. (#47296; thanks Aladiah96)

Languages

  • Added syntax highlighting for Go 1.27 generic methods. (#64562; thanks ndellosa95)

Other

  • Added an option to open README files by default by setting on_new_window to "readme". (#65389)
  • Improved the Project Panel error message when moving a file or folder onto a location where one with the same name already existed. (#64344; thanks zaugust4891)

Bug Fixes

  • Fixed Solargraph completions crashing Zed in certain cases. (#66157)
  • Fixed "Zed quit unexpectedly" appearing when quitting with cmd-q on macOS. (#66158)
  • Fixed a crash when adding surrounding characters (m s) to adjacent selections in Helix mode. (#66107; thanks bugprone)
  • Fixed child filesystem watches losing events when a covering parent watch was removed. (#65998)
  • Fixed collapsed inline diff hunks producing invalid word-diff highlights for multibyte UTF-8 text. (#65829; thanks Blazearth)
  • Fixed external agents' tool call locations opening one line below the line the agent reported. (#66110)
  • Fixed file links with line numbers failing to open WSL files when using Zed on Windows. (#66089; thanks buvidk1234)
  • Fixed Git Graph rendering lagging behind when opening or closing the commit details panel. (#66132; thanks RemcoSmitsDev)
  • Fixed inline review switching to a subagent's edits when the subagent started. (#66093)
  • Fixed issues detecting local and remote environment variables when using PowerShell (pwsh). (#66097)
  • Fixed language server formatting requests sometimes applying incorrect changes. (#65856)
  • Fixed language server setup failing with EBADDEVENGINES in Yarn and pnpm projects. (#65280; thanks latin-1)
  • Fixed language servers receiving change and save notifications for files outside their dynamically registered document selectors, including unrelated files in the project. (#65253; thanks aviatesk)
  • Fixed LLM credentials being read when disable_ai was set to true. (#66029)
  • Fixed missed filesystem events for case-insensitive watches registered with trailing separators or equivalent path spellings. (#65995)
  • Fixed missing Git repository, worktree, and branch names in the title bar for Windows remote projects opened in a repository subfolder. (#66142; thanks lingyaochu)
  • Fixed missing thinking output from OpenAI-compatible Responses endpoints that emitted reasoning-text events. (#65938)
  • Fixed on-type formatting after pressing enter being applied before the new line's indentation, which left the cursor after text inserted by language servers such as Ruby LSP. (#65769; thanks JacobNWolf)
  • Fixed remote projects (SSH, WSL) becoming unresponsive when loading an agent thread that referred to many files outside the project. (#65189; thanks coinman-dev)
  • Fixed renaming files, creating files, and renaming terminal tabs being cancelled when focus_follows_mouse was enabled and the mouse moved. (#66167)
  • Fixed retryable provider failures in OpenAI-compatible chat streams not being recognized as retryable. (#66038)
  • Fixed right-click menus and popup pickers closing when the mouse moved to another pane with focus_follows_mouse enabled. (#66173)
  • Fixed sandboxed agent terminal commands incorrectly reporting failure under dash on Linux. (#64439; thanks NHendrickson9616)
  • Fixed SSH connections failing when usernames containing / or \ were percent-encoded. (#64744; thanks ryanabx)
  • Fixed status bar panel buttons doing nothing when another panel in the same dock was zoomed. (#66119; thanks ybelmach)
  • Fixed streaming connection failures being classified as non-retryable errors for Google AI and OpenAI Responses model requests. (#65940)
  • Fixed the automatically installed Delve debugger for Go not updating after its initial installation. (#66025)
  • Fixed changes to focus_follows_mouse not applying to docks until Zed was restarted. (#66161)
  • Fixed deleted files remaining in the File Finder's history. (#65660)
  • Fixed duplicating files or folders in the Project Panel overwriting clipboard contents. (#60894; thanks shindgew)
  • Fixed external stash renames not being reflected in the stash picker. (#55294)
  • Fixed failed model-list fetches, such as those caused by invalid or expired API keys, being silently dropped instead of logged for Anthropic, Ollama, LM Studio, llama.cpp, OpenRouter, and Vercel AI Gateway. (#64171; thanks somtri)
  • Fixed language servers' dynamically registered capabilities applying to files outside their document selector's pattern. (#65252; thanks aviatesk)
  • Fixed long recent project names and the Open Project shortcut overflowing on the Welcome screen and in narrow panels. (#65531; thanks junkmailkeep)
  • Fixed pasting in Helix select mode with shift-r, space p, or cmd-v not returning to normal mode. (#65492; thanks raum-dellamorte)
  • Fixed project entries reappearing in Recent Projects after removal. (#66127; thanks EcutAtom336)
  • Fixed project names in the title bar for repo.git bare clones on remote Windows hosts. (#66199; thanks lingyaochu)
  • Fixed reordering a Markdown editor tab switching back to Markdown preview when "Open Markdown Files in Preview" was enabled. (#65795; thanks mateioprea)
  • Fixed running Go tests on Windows failing with an invalid-directory error. (#65722; thanks giocld)
  • Fixed scrollbar settings not applying to already open terminals, panels, and menus until they were reopened. (#66017)
  • Fixed unnecessary-code dimming sometimes disappearing when code_lens and semantic_tokens: "full" were both enabled. (#56003)

Breaking Changes and Notices

  • Removed support for macOS Catalina and Big Sur. Zed now requires macOS 12.0 (Monterey) or later. (#65994)
  • Removed agent checkpoints and the "Restore Checkpoint" button. Taking a checkpoint before every message made the agent slow to respond, or hang, in large Git repositories. To undo the agent's changes, reject its edits or use Git. (#65989)

This week's release includes Pyrefly as an optional built-in Python language server, the ability to expand and inspect nested variable values directly in debugger hovers, and DWARF debugging for WebAssembly sources in the JavaScript debugger.

Features

AI

  • Improved ACP configuration defaults to apply only to new conversations, preserving session settings when reopening existing conversations. (#65850)
